ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u1	Resume session. Senators, please take your seats. Senators of the secretary's office, please take your seats at the president's table. We proceed with the examination of item 10 of the agenda: information about the activities of the Institute of National Remembrance of the Commission for the Investigation of Crimes against the Polish Nationals during the period from 1 January to 31 December 2015. The text of the information is contained in print No 166. The Senate Marshal sent the information received to the Committee on Human Rights, Rule of Law and Petitions. At its meeting on 7 June 2016, the Commission heard the information and informed the Senate Marshal. I would like to welcome the President of the Institute of National Remembrance present at the Senate meeting of the Commission for the pursuit of Crime against the Polish Nationals, Mr Łukasz Kaminski. Welcome, Mr. President. I ask the President to take the floor and present the information. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u2	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Last year, which concerns the information presented today, the Institute of National Remembrance celebrated the 15th anniversary of its existence. And it was a natural time for us to take stock of some summaries, balance sheets, and it was also an opportunity to hear an opinion about the activities of the Institute of National Remembrance during these 15 years, we also listened to these opinions in this room. These opinions, this balance sheet is ♪ I'm not hiding ♪ For us it is a reason to be proud of all that we have achieved. But 2015 was above all a time of regular work and this is what the information presented today is about. It is extensive, but even such extensive information does not cover all our actions. I'm going to have to present them in a far-reaching shortcut. The largest part of the Institute of National Remembrance is our archive. The archive is also an essential element of the operation of all other branches of the Institute of National Remembrance, the entire Institute uses the activities of the archive. This archive currently counts 91 km of current files and increased last year by 450 m. So that despite the passage of years this archive is still growing, for two reasons. Firstly, we are still discovering in institutions which are legally obliged to pass on documents which are subject to transmission to the Institute of National Remembrance. Most of these documents last year were obtained from the structures of state administration, police, prison, army, prosecutors and courts. The second element that affects the expansion of the resource is the donations of individuals. We take over very important collections from people living in Poland, but also - What's more important? On an increasingly large scale we also take over collections that were created on emigration, thus contributing not only to their behaviour, but also to the sharing of them to Polish historians. Our archive has also been collecting copies of documents from the archives of foreign countries, documents concerning the history of Poland and Poles for many years. We have acquired over 650 000 pages of such documents in recent years. Last year we obtained them from archives of Estonia, Georgia, Ukraine and Hungary. Among these documents, which in the form of copies reach our archive, there are also documents from Polish institutions outside the borders. For many years we have supported the activities of Polish archives outside the country. In a special way, our support in organizing and digitising the collections concerns the Institute of Piłsudski in New York, the Polish Library in Paris, the Study of Polish Underground in London and the Polish Museum in Rapperswil, where last year we not only developed and digitized some collections, but also prepared a web portal and an exhibition together and an accompanying album devoted to Polish soldiers interned in Switzerland during World War II. Last year, our archive received a new task, resulting from the implementation of the Act on anti-communist opposition activists and oppressed persons, a law adopted on the initiative of the High House. This law entered into force on 31 August and by the end of the year, acting on the basis of it, we issued 735 administrative decisions and implemented 613 requests for evidence of opposition activity, which is a necessary step to ensure that the persons concerned can then apply to the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons for appropriate rights and in some cases also benefits. This action is similar to the action carried out for several years now in connection with the award of the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity. The Institute of National Remembrance is the only institution authorized to apply to the President of the Republic for the award of the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity. It is a distinction highly appreciated in the community of anti-communist opposition activists. Last year, we submitted 882 applications to the President, and the President granted one thousand and 78 awards, most of which were presented during ceremonies organized by the Institute of National Remembrance. This is not our statutory duty, this is the action that we took two years ago, on our own initiative, in order to make the celebrations of this award special. Last year we organized 30 such events. Since the beginning of the Institute of National Remembrance, the so-called public interest has aroused a great deal of interest. a restricted collection, a collection isolated from the archives of the Institute of National Remembrance. Today comes into force a law that eliminates this collection. But without waiting for this bill, and not even knowing that such a law would enter into force, we tried to actively cooperate with the special forces to reduce it. Last year this collection was reduced to the greatest extent in the history of the Institute of National Remembrance More than 6 000 800 archival units have been transferred to the public. I would also like to draw attention to the start, after a longer period of preparation, of the process of transferring to new magnetic tape carriers, which are contained in a proprietary set. These are the tapes we took from the Secret Service, but there were no devices that would allow them to be read. We managed to collect such devices in cooperation with the ABW, an attempt was made to read 401 tapes, out of which 263 were successfully read. Of course, the others we'll still be trying to read. The scale of activity of the archives of the Institute of National Remembrance gives the numbers. Last year 65 thousand applications were made for the provision of archive materials, various types of applications, and 271 000 checks were carried out for other branches of the Institute, for the purposes of the competent authorities of the State. Such a wide range of work is possible, among others. Thanks to the continuous modernisation of our archive, the introduction of the system of the Digital Archive, which is now, I dare say, the best system of archival information in Poland, thanks to the digitisation of the collections, which only last year covered 33 thousand archival units. The foundation of the Institute of National Remembrance, which makes the most of this good work of the archive, is a mirror pawn, one can say that to a large extent the work of the mirror pawn is dependent on cooperation with the archive. The Mirror Office of the Institute of National Remembrance gathered 371 000 illustrative statements at the end of last year, of which 56 thousand have so far been verified. Where does this huge discrepancy come from? First of all, the obligation to mirror candidates in local elections, not elected people, but those who simply ran. After each municipal election, this gap between verified statements and statements collected unfortunately increases. What we have managed to improve in previous years is the number of verified checks per year. Last year there were 9.5 thousand of them, which is almost double the increase over 5 years, with this growth achieved without any changes e.g. in employment, it's not like more people were assigned to this job because we didn't have that opportunity. This is achieved only by improving work and introducing new procedures, new solutions, including technical ones. The prosecutors of the IPN Mirror Division last year submitted 133 requests for the initiation of a mirror proceeding and 6 posts in the case of self-images. 168 final judgments have been taken, including 124 declaring that the claim was not accurate. The second pawn, prosecutorial, Institute of National Remembrance, is the pawn of prosecution of crimes against the Polish nation. In 2015, the prosecutors of this facility completed a thousand 78 investigations. The vast majority, because more than 60%, are investigations into communist crimes, 387 of German crimes, 25 of other crimes. And it is worth noting that this level of the number of investigations completed per year remains at a similar level with the decreasing level of the level of the criminal prosecution against the Polish nation. These investigations have, like, a few aspects, a few meanings. The first, and of course basic, is to bring justice to the perpetrators of the crimes committed, which is, of course, increasingly difficult as time passes. Last year, 14 indictments against 16 people were sent to the courts. What I would like to point out is that we managed to overcome the collapse that occurred in 2011 as a result of the decision of the Supreme Court, which changed the definition of limitation periods in the case of communist crimes, which resulted in the need to cancel hundreds of proceedings, hundreds of investigations which, I am convinced, would largely end in court. It is worth noting that most of these investigations, which I am talking about, have been going on from the beginning with the full awareness that it will be impossible to bring the perpetrators to trial, even if the perpetrators die. However, here is this statutory exception in the powers of our prosecutors, because, in so far as the prosecutor of the U.S. Attorney General in such a situation is required to cancel or not initiate an investigation, our prosecutors are obliged to conduct it in order to determine all the circumstances of the crime. I am convinced that this dimension of symbolic justice contained in the waiver provisions is also important, because in such a decision to terminate the investigation, the prosecutor most often determines the legal qualification of the crime and, if possible, identifies the responsible and identifies the victims. So that it also has a legal dimension, because these materials from proceedings, they happen, they serve, for example. in various types of civil proceedings related to the investigation of various claims e.g. The victims' families. And I am convinced that even if these investigations were completed at the moment 100% without bringing the indictment to the courts, i.e. in cases such as: German crimes or Soviet crimes, that these acts of redemption, precisely with this good legal argument, will still have their meaning in the future, apart from such obvious meaning, means that in this way important source materials are also created for further historical research. Because prosecutors have powers that are not available to researchers, and only last year 4,000 70 witnesses were interviewed, and one day this material will be available to historians. It is also worth paying attention to the importance of these investigations for loved ones, for families. There are many such investigations that arouse the interest of the nearest victims. I'm just going to give you one example: an investigation into an Augustan manhunt with a lot of interest. This is a difficult investigation, because we cannot count on legal cooperation from the Russian side, nor, unfortunately, as it turns out, the Belarusian side, and yet one step at a time we manage to draw closer to the truth. Last year, which we are talking about today I will mention only two events • prosecutors received a very broad opinion from experts based on the analysis of aerial photographs from the 1940s, 1950s and modern satellite photos, which allowed to identify the likely burial sites of the victims. And by monitoring various types of Russian websites, contrary to the official statements in the official documents of the Russian party, that there are no documents on the raid in the Russian archives, we were able to obtain copies of military documents presenting the military aspects of this great crime. Prosecutors of the Institute of National Remembrance are also active participants in the project, which in recent years arouses the greatest interest of the public, i.e. the project of searching for unknown burial sites of victims of communist terror. This project is run primarily by the Independent Department of Search for IPN. Last year these works were carried out in many places, I will mention only those which ended in finding the remains of the victims of the Bródnowski Cemetery in Warsaw, Gdansk, Płock, Rzeszów, Tarnów and Białystok Cemetery. Białystok is a special place, the area near the arrest of investigators, the former prison, because in search of victims of communist terror, we also found victims of German terror from the time of World War II, which I believe is an eloquent symbol of the fate of Poles in the 20th century. The total remains of 385 people have already been found and throughout the project since autumn 2011. More than 800 people, 66 of whom are already identified. Last year there was a solemn funeral of the first 35 persons identified from the Powazkowska Kojakka. A law has also entered into force that solves the difficult problem of carrying later graves. The Institute of National Remembrance submitted applications for the transfer of graves from the accommodation unit As far as Powązki is concerned, as far as I know, all decisions have already been issued by the voivode, the first graves are being moved so that this problem will finally be closed in the near future. Another area of activity of the Institute of National Remembrance is research carried out within 11 nationwide projects and 14 branch projects. 4 national projects by decision of the Council are of a priority nature. These are the projects: the Polish lands under occupation 1939-1945, the government of the Polish People's Republic in the face of socio-political crises and the democratic opposition 1956-1976. In recent years, this project focused on the events of 1956, the Structures and activities of the Polish People's Republic of Poland and the Polish political emigration 1939-1990. It is worth noting that researchers from outside the Institute of National Remembrance also participate in all these national projects. We also try to encourage representatives of other centres, especially universities, centres of the Polish Academy of Sciences, to cooperate with us in the implementation of these projects. Research results are presented at scientific conferences. Last year we organised 53 scientific conferences, 17 of which are international, and 23 popularization sessions. The Institute also conducts large documentary projects, implemented in the form of a competition for NGOs. These are the Poles' Index murdered and repressed for helping Jews, as well as the personal losses and victims of repression under German occupation. We are also conducting a notation project, recording long accounts of witnesses of history. Last year, 173 such accounts were recorded. Research is also the basis for publishing, although it is worth remembering that many of our publications are also popular in science. In the past year 195 publications were published, which, if I am not mistaken, is the highest number in the history of the Institute, and in addition 40 older publications were made available in the Digital Library in electronic form. This Digital Library is growing from year to year. From the educational publications I will mention maybe only three: another educational game of the institute,.7. In the defense of Lviv, published in Polish, English and Ukrainian languages; a series of comic books by Antek Silver's war odyssey, also enjoying huge popularity, for example at the book fair, is at the moment the largest publishing hit of the Institute of National Remembrance; and the popular synthesis of a hundred years of scouting. As far as publications of a more scientific nature are concerned, just as an example I will mention the post of the presidents of the Republic of Poland in exile, which we published on the 25th anniversary of the transmission of insignia by President Kaczorowski. Besides, the presentation of this publication took place in the Senate, and thank you for this opportunity. Last year, the first volume appeared, but in 2 volumes, a new, extended, improved, built-in scientific apparatus of the edition of the fundamental work of the National Army in documents. This is a project that's been going on for several years. It was initiated by the sleeper. Chairman Janusz Kurtyka. We were able to finish the first volume. The next ones will appear, I think, faster. It is an extremely important publishing house. And another, whose first volumes may begin to appear this year, initiated last year, of a similar, I think, meaning, is the editing of Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński's notes. We have signed an agreement with the relevant ecclesiastical institutions to issue them in the form of 30 volumes, of course also in the scientific study. From such publications, which were of great interest to readers, I will exchange the biography of Michał Żymierski and maybe the book on monuments of gratitude This is a topic that last year became particularly hot, and the Institute of National Remembrance was fortunate to have published a very thorough study describing this problem. The form of recognition for the publication of the Institute of National Remembrance is numerous awards, which our books receive in practically every historical competition, for historical books that take place in Poland, with the exception of the "Historical Book of the Year," which we organize, so it is difficult to promote our own publications there. Educational activities, perhaps the most recognizable sphere of activity of the Institute of National Remembrance, are certainly particularly important. Each year thousands of educational projects are carried out, from one-off projects in the form of lessons for a group of students to year-round projects that involve hundreds or even thousands of people. Our educational activity is addressed not only to students, but also to teachers and ♪ What to remember ♪ ♪ What to remember ♪ ♪ What to remember ♪ ♪ It's for the general public. A large part of our offer is addressed to all Poles, not just to the young generation. As far as the offer for teachers is concerned, in addition to many trainings which I will not talk about, I will mention a new educational briefcase, a new educational material devoted to the Polish independence underground. This is the material that teachers have long been waiting for because of the increased interest in the history of Soldiers Cursed among young people. I would like to mention only a few of the many projects aimed at young people. The first is a group of projects. These are educational projects, I'll tell you about free Poland, the Stones of Memory, you can't forget about it... A meeting with women who went through the hell of camps and camps, equipment and buttons with an eagle of rust. These are projects, from my own personal point of view, particularly important, because they are not based on the fact that students are simply meant to acquire some knowledge from one publication or another. These are projects where young people discover history by themselves looking for sources, often meeting with living witnesses of history. And that is why, I am convinced, these projects go to them much more than traditional competitions, where you just have to prepare yourself, then complete some test or solve some tasks, because they are able to feel certain emotions and get to know the emotions of people participating in events that students describe. But of course we also engage in traditional ones, such as the historical Olympics. For several years we have been a partner of the Polish Historical Society in the organization of the historical Olympics. I would like to draw attention to the Oxford debates, which last year for the first time on a national scale were a form of educational activity of the Institute of National Remembrance. This is, of course, a very elite action aimed at a relatively narrow group of young people who form teams and later in mode, I would say, the cups face each other, are selected leaders in particular regions who later meet in Warsaw. This is not only a school of understanding history, but also a school of public debate. I am convinced that there are probably and future senators among the participants in the Oxford debates. And I would also like to encourage your senators to participate, because it is today and tomorrow that the final of the Oxford debate tournament is held for the Cup of the President of the IPN. It is really worth a moment to listen to these young people who are often in a difficult situation, because the idea of the Oxford debate is that they do not choose whether to defend themselves or to oppose it. You have to be able to find arguments, sometimes against your own beliefs, so it really is a good thinking school. In our educational activity we also reach for the film. Let me just remind you that last year we had the sixth edition of the film review of Echa Katynia, which was already held in 30 cities. From year to year, this event includes more and more localities. Of course, we use the Internet, educational portals. The greatest of them, Memory. en, last year had 2 million 500 thousand entrances. Last year four new portals were created. There's also our YouTube channel where we put our film production. Last year, we expanded on a much larger scale than before, the idea of "History Stop." Because we know that in many towns, where there is no branch or IPN delegation, there is a demand for a meeting with the institute, a meeting with historians, we create the Stops of History. They, unlike here in Warsaw, do not have their headquarters, where something happens every day, but regularly, once or twice a month, there are such initiatives. Last year we prepared 20 new inserts, the most popular of which, when they silenced, cried out, was shown in 38 localities. We also continue to show exhibitions from years ago that are of interest. Last year we showed 186 such older exhibitions, many of them many times. We're working with TV. Together with the television, the Republic carries out a series of documentary films from the security film, and with the Polish Television we realized two programs in the previous year: History for you and the birth of Solidarity, as well as an age-long test from history, which had over 2 million viewers. It is one of the largest audiences in terms of the great tests carried out by Program 1. And maybe the last element of the educational activity, but quite current: the project of the patrons of our streets. In addition to leading our, probably to the well-known, long-standing struggle to change those, so to speak, bad patrons connected with the communist system, we also try to promote patrons who deserve it, but also explain to Poles who were people who were patrons of their street. Because it is unfortunately a common phenomenon that such knowledge is rather moderate. So we prepare special brochures, which are most often supplied by scouts, by members of various types of student historical clubs. E.g. brochures are distributed on Pileckiego Street. And this, of course, is an opportunity for me to thank the High House once again for bringing forward a second legislative initiative that will finally solve this problem. We are already preparing, as an institute, for the entry into force of the law. We want not only to wait for the moment when we will issue opinions at the request of the voivods, but already to advise local governments on what street names should be changed. And of course, if necessary, we will also advise on who to choose as a new patron. Especially from local heroes. Because since we're talking about over a thousand street names that should change, it's a great opportunity to remind a lot of forgotten people. Not only in the national dimension, but also in the dimension of creating a certain local identity. The Institute of National Remembrance, as every year, was also active outside the country. This activity took place in several fields. One of them is related to the dissemination of Polish experience in settlements with the past. For many years now, we have seen that the Polish model has a great deal of interest, there is a great need for study visits to the institute and for the trips of our experts to other countries to share this experience. We also work with our partner institutions around the world. This is both bilateral cooperation... Last year it was particularly intense with the Hungarian National Remembrance Committee, this is a new institution. The results of this cooperation will be collected in the current year, jointly celebrating the anniversary of the events of 1956 in Poland and Hungary. This is also multilateral cooperation, mainly within two institutions. The first is the European Network of Archive Institutions dealing with Secret Service Files. The greatest effect of this cooperation was the exhibition, which was created mainly thanks to the Institute of National Remembrance, devoted to how the communist security apparatus, the secret police, influenced everyday life in the countries of the Soviet bloc, which was opened in October 2015 in Berlin. And the second institution is the Platform of European Remembrance and Conscience. Here I will just mention that my deputy, Dr. Paweł Ukielski, was elected for the second time last year to the authorities of this organization, bringing together more than 50 institutions and organisations from all over Europe. Another area is historical dialogue. Here, of course, in recent years the most important is the Polish-Ukrainian dialogue. Last year the Polish-Ukrainian Historical Forum was established, established by the Polish Institute of National Remembrance and the Ukrainian Institute of National Remembrance. This dialogue has begun. It's very difficult, but it's rolling. I think it is an essential condition for a future real reconciliation between our peoples. Another area of activity abroad is support for Poles outside the country. I've already mentioned support for archival institutions. I will supplement this by saying that the second basic area of this activity is support for educational institutions, for Polish schools operating outside the country. This support is carried out both through the provision of educational material and through training, which is probably extremely important. We train Polish teachers, inviting them to Poland, but also send our educators e.g. for Polish educational conventions in America, where there is an opportunity to conduct workshops for a large group of people of this type. The last area is the dissemination of knowledge about Polish history outside the country. This is an important element of the recent public debate on the protection of good name. In my opinion, this foundation must be an active activity for knowledge of Polish history. I've already talked about international scientific conferences. Our historians participate in conferences abroad This is a form of reaching out to the scientific community, which is important for the formulation of these ideas. But we also try to work with teachers. Last year there was a second training for 25 history teachers from Europe called Poland at the heart of European history. We are trying to show Polish perspective on the most important events in the history of the 20th century. We are active on the Internet, some of our portals have foreign Internet versions. Last year, I would like to stress, another two language versions of the Truth About Camps portal were added. This is a portal which in such a very short, but serious form presents the history of German concentration camps, German extermination camps in occupied Poland. The context of this portal is obvious. As an example of another form of activity in the dissemination of Polish history outside the country, I would like to mention the announcement that we managed to include in the Komsomol Truth. This year, it was not possible to repeat it, but last year, once, on the 70th anniversary of the kidnapping and trial of 16 leaders of the Underground Poland, we placed an announcement on this subject, which reflected quite a lot of interest in Russia. Even if someone was negative about it, at least he knew some basic facts. Ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo, in a few days it will be 16 years since the Institute of National Remembrance exists. All this time we try in various forms, often changing them under the influence of experience, to carry out the same basic mission that has been set before us. A mission that I believe is best expressed in the preamble to the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance. These are words that we try to remember quite often. Allow me to quote them. In creating the Institute of National Remembrance, the parliament of the Republic of Poland had in mind: to preserve the memory of the great sacrifices, losses and damages suffered by the Polish people during and after the Second World War; the patriotic traditions of the struggle between the Polish people and the invaders, Nazism and communism; the actions of citizens in favour of the independent existence of the Polish State and in defence of freedom and human dignity; the obligation to prosecute crimes against peace, humanity and war crimes, and the obligation to make amends by our country to all those who have been wronged by the state violating human rights. It was also held that no unlawful action by the State against citizens could be protected by a secret or be forgotten. This mission, the work of the Institute of National Remembrance, is primarily aimed at the past, but I believe that it is also an important factor in the co-formation of our present, and in fact many elements of our activity are directed towards the future. What the Institute of National Remembrance is doing is primarily fulfilling our common duty to the heroes of Polish history and to the victims of totalitarian systems. The formation of memory is a fundamental element of shaping the Polish identity, because we will not understand ourselves as a nation without knowing our own past. But this past also allows us to understand the present, to understand contemporary Poland, to understand the modern world. An important element of our work is the formation of attitudes. We resemble the attitudes of people who opposed totalitarian systems, opposed the invaders. I am convinced that this is how we shape our attitudes and, you can say, we even make certain demands, and not only to the representatives of the young generation. I think this memory of the past carries some commitments for all of us. And it carries something else, something very important. Speaking of the past of Poland in the 20th century, we are really talking about values, values accompanying people we remember. These are the values we need equally today in our contemporary Polish life. And it's not just the values that we try to show to others as the Institute of National Remembrance. These are the values on which we try to base our daily work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

As for the proprietary collection, because of the amount of materials that left this collection last year, which is about half of everything that came out of the reserved collection from the beginning, I can actually operate only with certain categories, not with specific examples. The first category, the biggest one, I think, or one of the bigger ones, is the officers' personnel files. It is evident that in the creation of this proprietary set a lot of documentation concerning the former officers was placed there. Another group are various types of materials concerning the internal functioning of the Security Service, personal orders, the functioning of certain parts of the operational fund, because a large part of it has been in the public collection for a long time. And finally, third category These are people's work. Of course, there is also a group of agents, i.e. secret co-workers of various branches of the communist security apparatus. If I were to try to draw any conclusions from the analysis of those materials that left the restricted collection last year, the basic conclusion is that it is really difficult to understand how these materials fulfilled the statutory definition that allowed it to be included in the restricted collection, and I would remind you that it states that there could be material relevant to the security of the State. But in my opinion legal measures are also needed, although I have a great doubt about criminal measures as such, because these violations are 99% outside Poland, so real prosecution It's not symbolic, but it's really almost impossible. Any rule of international legal exchange assumes that it is possible to prosecute nationals of other countries, but when the offence in question is also in the criminal code of that State. Otherwise, no state will allow its citizen to be prosecuted. Besides, let's pay attention to the past few years. The loudest statements were those of Barack Obama and James Comey, the head of the FBI. It's hard to imagine us chasing the leader of a friendly state and the head of one of the largest police and intelligence services in the world. Therefore, I believe that the main focus should be on civil means, civil processes, not criminal laws, just the use of civil laws, the defence of good names, the defence of good, which is a certain national dignity, etc. And here it is necessary to move on to the second question The cooperation between the various institutions. Without the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, this cannot be done because the Ministry has the largest network to collect information about this type of case and this first signaling, enabling reaction. But the Institute of National Remembrance is also needed to provide substantive support in such cases. Examples of the successes that the senator was talking about. So that in my opinion it should be just such a triangle, i.e. at least two institutions of the State of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and IPN and a strong social position. As I understand it, we're going a little bit this way, because the regulations that were prepared at the Ministry of Justice were more civil, although certain criminal laws were also introduced. It was, for example. o provisions enabling the Institute of National Remembrance to engage in civil processes as a supporting party. And the last decisions of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage, that is, cooperation with one of the international law firms, is also a step in that direction, that it is necessary to go towards civil processes rather than inefficient prosecution of foreigners on the basis of Polish criminal laws.

We proceed with the examination of item 5 of the agenda: the Act amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources and some other laws. The text of the Act is contained in print No 200 and the committee report In print No 200 A. I would like to ask the rapporteur of the Committee on National Economy and Innovation and the Committee on the Environment, Senator Grzegorz Pekkis, to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u89	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The overarching objective of the amendments to the Act is sustainable development based on the concept of energy clusters and energy cooperatives. In this respect, increasing energy production in clusters and energy cooperatives will further increase the use of local resources, improve energy security in rural areas, increase the pace of development of these areas, which have so far been economically neglected. The proposed solutions remove barriers that discourage the creation of energy cooperatives and other forms of cooperation aimed at jointly meeting energy needs. Another major change is the development of the use of local biomass. Effective use of biomass available in the vicinity of manufacturing units, in particular waste and residues from the production processes of wood, paper, furniture, agri-food, etc. and reducing the transport of biomass over long distances will, on the one hand, create new jobs in rural areas and, on the other hand, contribute to the efficiency of the use of national energy resources. Effective use of industrially unfit wood... The result will be a new definition of energy wood. It will increase the possibility of using wood for energy purposes due to low quality characteristics which prevent it from being used in industry. The Act designes mechanisms to initiate the proper development of prosumer installations. Changes in the development of micro-installations of renewable energy sources aim to develop a prosumer civil movement aimed at the creation of renewable energy installations with a view to meeting its own energy needs rather than generating high profits. The proposed changes consist of abandoning the idea of introducing fixed guaranteed tariffs, which focus on creating a financial incentive to produce maximum electricity, not the quantity necessary to meet their own energy needs prosumers. In place of the guaranteed tariffs, support is proposed in the form of the quantitative settlement of energy entered into and collected by prosumers under the concluded comprehensive agreements. Such a solution will enable prosumer to use the electricity grid as a kind of energy storage. The amendments proposed at this stage mostly relate to the drafting corrections of the adopted legal text. The remaining amendments in the area of the obligation to purchase the offered heat from the thermal treatment plant and other renewable energy installations are intended to improve the current state of play. Another amendment removes the cases of blocking the possibility of connecting cheap and stable renewable energy sources to district heating networks. Therefore, the recommendation that energy undertakings and district heating companies in the area of a given district heating network should purchase the offered heat generated by the connected installations, but not more than the demand of the end-users of that company connected to the network. What is equally important, the energy company will be required to agree to the connection e.g. geothermal installations for heating networks. The connection will be implemented in accordance with the provisions of energy law, taking into account the provisions of the Energy Minister's Regulation. Until this regulation is adopted, the existing rules will be maintained. The Commission has been meeting with experts. The discussion was quite detailed, attended by both experts and the President of the Energy Regulatory Office present at the committee meeting. The discussion was very thematically focused. I think that the valuable questions asked during the committee's work, the sensible questions, the answers were answered and the doubts were clarified.

The difference that this law introduces in relation to the previous provisions is that the State does not remove the tools for industrial policy and social policy. Therefore, auctions are divided thematically. And here, which is very important, it is not only the price that is considered important. Because we all have to realize one thing: the energy system doesn't just matter about energy, but it also matters that it is delivered at the moment when someone needs it. There are sources that generate or have the ability to generate energy as a result of certain events For example, when the sun shines or the wind blows And there are sources that can supply that energy at their disposal, so when someone needs that energy. Not reflecting this in the energy system leads to trouble, and sometimes to a counterproductive outcome. I will draw your attention to the statistics on Germany, a very developed country with regard to RES, which generated more carbon dioxide at the beginning of its policy than before it was introduced. Why? Well, precisely because the maintenance of the energy supply was carried out through conventional sources that were activated ad hoc It wasn't their characterization. And they generated this carbon dioxide. We want to avoid such mistakes. Germany has also begun to deal with this, better or worse, but they have switched to other energy sources, which they believe are strategically beneficial. It would be too difficult for us to accept natural gas on the same scale, to say the least, because it is not our raw material and its cause depends on certain political factors.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u153	Thank you very much. Please, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u154	Thank you so much for asking. Senator, the biogas plant isn't equal. There are biogas plants, which are the natural end of a certain process, usually of some kind, are biogas plants based on waste, the existence of which is due to some processes occurring in a given place, and are biogas plants, which are created in anticipation of the fact that the crops will be supplied. Therefore, there are dilemmas, in my opinion very difficult to resolve. Example. Someone who owned a biogas plant at the same time had quite a powerful field, about 100 ha, where he grew corn. This corn is suitable for use in the biogas plant, but the prices of maize have increased, so it is more profitable to sell it. In this situation, this biogas plant is close to the pot, because there's nothing to be powered. How can we solve such problems? Actually, there aren't any good ideas. We are trying to direct the interest of this segment of industry towards the creation of biogas plants as a coherent element of the local economy, where there are indeed some needs for waste management. We believe that these support mechanisms, which are now being initiated in the law, will support these situations to a sufficient extent. But surely there will be a catalogue of situations that we have not foreseen and whose support could only be dictated by the fact that it is also RES energy. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u227	Mr Marshal, ladies and gentlemen of the Senators, I would like to propose an amendment to the Act amending the Act on Renewable Energy Sources and some other laws. Art. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 is replaced by the following: "The Minister responsible for energy matters shall, by means of a regulation, amend the share referred to in Article. 59 Act as amended by Art. 1 for 2017, but not less than 17% , taking into account the nature of the undertaking, the amount of electricity produced in micro-installations and small installations, the obligations arising from international agreements, the current share of electricity and renewable energy fuels in total energy and fuel consumed in energy and transport, the amount of biogas produced and the price of electricity in the competitive market, by 30 November 2016. The Energy Minister may once again reduce the share referred to in Article. 59 Act as amended by Art. 1 for 2021. Between 2018 and 2020 the amount of the share referred to in Article. 59 Act as amended by Art. 1. is 20% It's okay, it's okay, it's okay, it's okay. The amendment aims to restore balance in the market for certificates of origin, i.e. green certificates. The incorrect decisions and omissions of the regulatory centre in the previous mandate have resulted in a significant oversupply of certificates of origin currently on the market, more than 20TWh, which already exceeds their annual obligation to be remitted by energy suppliers to final customers for 2016. Thus, the proposals of the provisions contained in the draft RES Act consisting of a mandatory change, or in fact a de facto reduction of the obligation to waive green certificates in 2017 below the level laid down in the Law of 20 February 2015 are a decisive step which aggravates the market situation of certificates of origin. It is difficult to predict what reduction the Minister may accept, and from the models available on the market for market developments in green certificates it is clear that oversupply of certificates of origin will increase and the price of certificates of origin will decrease unless the obligation to waive certificates of origin is maintained at 20%. in the following years, i.e. 2017/12021. Contrary to frequent comments that support for renewable energy sources has a significant impact on energy prices for final customers, RES industry experts show that if an increase in the average price of a certificate of origin is assumed to be PLN 200 per MWh from PLN 100 per MWh, this could charge the sale of electricity to the final consumer of PLN 14 per MWh. For the average household, this would mean an increase in energy bills of 3 zł per month this year, that is about 2%. This is a theoretical estimate relating only to the price impulse associated with the increase in price and the number of green certificates to be redeemed. In practice, this impulse may be irrelevant or hidden in other factors of energy price change. It is worth noting that despite a close threefold decrease in the price of certificates of origin after 2011, from about 300 PLN for 1 MWh to less than 100 PLN and now it amounts to 80 PLN and at the same time almost 20% decrease in wholesale energy prices for final customers after 2011 the price of energy remains at the same or even slightly increasing level. It is therefore difficult to talk about the direct impact of increasing the obligation to reduce the price of energy, unless correlation occurs in only one direction. The current cost of RES support is 3,7% the total cost of electricity for the final customer. I myself was a participant in the conference and the amendments tabled The five of them are the result of many questions and doubts of the participants of this conference. Amendment 1 concerns Article. 1 point (2) (a) g. After point 16, the following point 16a is inserted: installed PV modules or energy converters (e.g. inverters) adjusting the electrical parameters of energy to the requirements of the electricity grid. Reason. Total installed power Electric is one of the most important parameters that classify renewable energy installations in the Act. This capacity is determined by the support system granted to the installation and many other requirements related to connection to the network and further operation. The lack of precise definition is too broad a scope for interpretation and abuse. The proposed definition is very universal, covering the specificities of different RES technologies, while at the same time being very simple and intuitive. The example cited in the photovoltaics provides an even better interpretation of the intentions of the legislator. Amendment 2. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 in point 3, at the point giving a new wording to the article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. The words "for three-phase micro-installations' are deleted. Reason. The limitation of full interphase energy balancing to three-phase micro-installations will hit the poorest prosumers who want to have low-power RES installations below 4 kW. In such cases, it is technically impossible or completely unjustified to build a three-phase micro-installation. In the case of a single phase micro-installation of RES and balancing separately in each prosumer phase, there will be limited possibilities to increase energy consumption, which is one of the main intentions of the legislator. As the home receivers are so distributed over each power supply phase that they are a relatively uniform load on the electricity grid, this is due to the conditions of connection to the network. The phase to which the micro-installation is attached cannot be artificially loaded, because if it does not function there will be an uneven load on the house's power phases. Amendment 3. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 1 (3) at the point giving the new wording of Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 11 delete words... Paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 11 is replaced by the following: "A surplus of the amount of electricity put into the grid by a prosumer in relation to the amount of energy it collects from the network under the waste management system shall be purchased by the seller referred to in Article. Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1a, at a price equal to the average energy price on the competitive market announced by the President of the URE for the period covered by the balance sheet. The mouth is removed. 12. Reason. If the surplus not consumed by a prosumer of energy were the revenue of the energy seller, it would be highly likely that the European Commission would consider it unacceptable to state aid. For Prosumers, waste savings will have a much higher value than revenues from wholesale energy sales, as in a competitive market. Therefore, you should not be afraid of deliberately over-dimension of the RES installation. According to all calculations, even a 15-year period of use of waste disposal systems will not result in prosumers returning the expenditure incurred on the construction of micro-installations. Leaving prosumers after this period without any sources of revenue, apart from saving energy consumed immediately without adding it to the grid, will make this situation even worse. In addition, in the social perception, the energy salesman's capture of all the surplus produced by the prosumer will be regarded as the most ordinary theft, and this will undermine the citizen's confidence in the institutions of the Republic of Poland. The costs to be incurred by the energy vendor in connection with the Prosumer Micro-Installation of RES should be financed by the RES fee. The argument that in order to sell electricity, a prosumer must carry out an economic activity is not in fact covered. This possibility has existed since the amendment of the Energy Law in 2003, i.e. the so-called "energy law." A little three-pack of energy. I was never required of individuals to start business with a view to selling surplus electricity produced. Prosumers account for energy sales revenue in annual PIT statements, such as other income, for example from the rental of apartments. Amendment 4. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 2 (3) (a), (b), (c), (c), (d), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (e), (f), (f), (f), (f), (f), (f), (h), (h), (h), (h), (h), (h), (h), (h), (h), In paragraph 8d11: Any load reduction or disconnect from the micro-installation network must be recorded by the settlement and measurement system (s) taking into account the duration and level of load reduction (or disconnect from the network) of the micro-installation. And in the mouth. 8d12: The owner shall be entitled to compensation for the time during which there was a reduction in the load on the micro-installation or its disconnect from the network. In the context of compensation, it is assumed that, during the limitation of the load on the micro-installation (or its disconnect from the network), it could operate with full total installed electrical power and that the electricity not introduced into the grid for this reason is included in the discharge system described in Article. 4 as part of the network. Reason. The amendment aims to combine the security requirements of electricity networks with the rights of owners of micro-installations. Taking into account the right of the OSD to temporarily reduce the power introduced from micro-installations to the network, in the event of a threat to its security, it requires that such situations be accurately recorded and compensated for by these activities to the owners of micro-installations. Since the owners of micro-installations are not responsible for the state of the transmission networks, they cannot indirectly, by limiting the generation capacity of their micro-installations, bear the costs of mismatching the network to the connected RES microsources. The granting of the proposed compensation on the one hand is an adequate compensation for limiting the generation capacity of micro-installations, and on the other hand it is a signal to the OSD to ensure that reductions in the load of micro-installations or their disconnect from the network are applied only as a last resort. The proposed compensation scheme can be compared with the so-called charges. preparedness in the case of conventional energy. Finally, Amendment 5. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 1 (3) at the end of the text describing the wording of Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 10 add the words "and shall not be subject to excise duty." Reason. The amendment is editorial in nature, explaining the intention of the legislator without having to check the provisions of the excise duty Act. The amendment will facilitate the understanding and interpretation of the provisions of paragraph 1. 10. Thank you very much, Marshal. I'm handing over the amendments. ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u257	Thank you, Mrs. Marshal. High Senate, I have the honour to present on behalf of the Senate Health Commission a bill amending the Act on Medical Activity and some other laws. The Act was examined at a meeting of the Health Committee on 13 June. The Commission asks that the law be adopted without amendment. Mrs Marshal, High Senate, the bill is quite extensive, so I will concentrate on the essential content of this law. This law is called the Cleaning Act, which is a law that affects various aspects of the healing activity. This law introduces amendments relating to the occurrence of the so-called. net losses, that is, what we used to call a negative financial result, an independent public health care facility, in short called SPOZ. The purpose of this change is that... the previous solution was of little effectiveness. Therefore, the change will be such that the net loss, as defined in Article. Paragraph 56 Point 2 (1) of that law will be covered by an establishment fund which will be reduced by that net loss. If the bet fund is not sufficient to cover that net loss, the entity creating the bet will be obliged to cover the remaining part of that loss, but not more than the amount resulting from the net loss and depreciation costs. The deadline for covering this loss will be 9 months from the expiry of the deadline for the approval of the accounts of the public health establishment itself. The extension of this deadline to 9 months will allow the planning of funds to cover net losses in the budgets of the competent authorities, i.e. entities forming the SPOZ. In order to improve this process, apart from the fact that this loss has to be covered... The Act obliges the manager of a self-employed public health establishment within 3 months of the expiry of the deadline for approval of the financial statements, i.e. until 30 September, to draw up a resolution programme for a period of not more than 3 years. The corrective programme must gain acceptance of the entity forming the SPOZ. The introduction of an obligation to draw up and implement a resolution programme strengthens the responsibility of the healthcare provider's manager for its financial results and will contribute to the improvement of a rational financial economy. The mere transfer of funds is almost insufficient, as we can only cover this net loss and not eliminate the cause of this loss. The second possible action of the entity that creates, apart from covering the loss of the SPOZ, is unfortunately the liquidation of the medicinal entity, of course, when this loss is not covered. This law changes what was in the previous, i.e. the solution is deleted, that if we do not cover the losses, then the plant will be converted into the so-called. the partnership. We are back to previous solutions that were before 2011. According to the Minister's presentation at the meeting of the Health Committee, the level of commitments for 2015 amounted to nearly PLN 11 billion and the level of commitments due was approximately PLN 2 billion. At the same time, we know that more than 70% The SPOZ regulated its obligations on a timely basis and did not show any liabilities due. The analysis of the financial results indicated that over 60% in 2012 and 2014 The SPOZ generated net profits. Thus, the financial situation of the SPOZ varies and I dare say that this is related to the condition of the management of the SPOZ. The next amendment introduced by this law is the prohibition on the disposal of shares or shares in companies of the Treasury or local government units if, as a result of their disposal, these entities lose a majority holding or shares. This regulation allows a public entity, i.e. a local government unit or State Treasury, to dispose of shares or shares of its companies provided that the nominal value of their shares or shares is not less than 51% the share capital of the company and that these objects will have the direct majority of the votes at the shareholders' meeting or at the general meeting. This solution will prevent the transfer of shares or shares of capital companies of the State Treasury or local government units carrying out medical activities. Having a majority stake or shares and controlling the company's organs will give public entities a decisive influence on the functioning of medicinal products, which is necessary to carry out the tasks of health security, quality and availability of health services under Article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In view of these conditions, the Act restricts the ownership of shares or shares of capital companies of the State Treasury or local government units carrying out medical activities, but this limitation does not affect the substance of their ownership, as they may continue to exercise their right in this respect, up to 49%. Capital companies carrying out medicinal activities are not created to benefit from the ownership of their shares or shares, but to provide the necessary health benefits. The measures taken before the date of entry into force of the Act aimed at the disposal of shares or shares of companies of the State Treasury and local government units will be subject to the existing provisions and therefore the Act does not infringe the so-called provisions. the interests in progress and thereby do not confer on the right of ownership under Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1st Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The Act will also allow the possibility to dispose of shares or shares of a company operating in the field of health care in favour of capital companies, to which the State Treasury has control. This solution aims to enable the State Treasury which was considered by the Minister of Treasury to include State Treasury companies providing health care to an entity in which the State Treasury's share in the share capital was less than 51%. The next amendment introduced by the Act is the principle of non-payment of dividends in capital companies in which the Treasury, local government unit or medical university has more than 51% shares or shares. It is proposed that no dividend should be paid in these entities, but that, in view of the protection of acquired rights, the protection of ownership and equity in respect of the law, this regulation would not apply to capital companies where, at the date of entry into force of the law, those entities did not have 100% of the shares or shares. This regulation will apply for the first time after the approval of the financial statements for the first financial year started after the date of entry into force of that law. Another regulation introduced by this law is the creation of tools for the supervision of an entity forming the independent public ZOZ. This is quite important regulation because the law requires the manager of the entity or the manager of the SP of the ZOZ to draw up a report on the economic and financial situation of the SP of the ZOZ. Such a report shall be transmitted to the formation entity by 31 May each year. The basis for the report for the previous financial year will be a model which will be prepared by the Minister of Health in agreement with the Minister of Finance. This report will describe the detailed economic and financial situation and will be based on an assessment of this situation. It will have to be submitted to the authority that runs the SPOZ. In our view, these measures will reduce the level of sovereign debt of public health establishments. Another option introduced by this law is the definition of the control procedure carried out by the voivodship in the event of the disposal by the provider of medicinal products, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses or medical devices, contrary to the prohibition under the Act of 6 September 2001 on pharmaceutical law. In general, the point is that the authority that should control this has not had the right instruments to do so, there was no regulation to determine the mode of such control. It is therefore proposed to clarify this by referring to the provisions of the Article. 111 and Article 4 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013. 112. Another amendment is to clarify the rules on the conduct of controls on medicinal products. The aim of this amendment is to harmonise the rules governing the scope of the control activities carried out by the Minister of Health and by the bodies to which the Minister of Health has commissioned such control, e.g. By the voivode. Another amendment, which is enshrined in this Act, concerns the transfer of the property of a public health establishment to a free use. As so far, the creator will be able to transfer free use to the independent public ZOZ... He will be able to transfer these properties to his property or under another legal title. Regardless of the way in which the property is transferred in accordance with the proposed provision, the SPOZ will manage this property. This is quite important because the transfer of buildings or land results in an increase in the initial fund and a greater liquidity of the SPOZ. The next regulation contained in this law introduces principles of cooperation between medical agents and the police as part of the search for missing persons. And here's two lines. First, the obligation for medical professionals to notify the police of the fact that patients are admitted to the hospital without providing data that could identify the person concerned. Secondly, the obligation for operators to provide information on the admission of a missing person within the meaning of the provisions of the Police Act. The obligation to inform the police of the admission of a patient would apply to patients with minors with whom a statutory representative cannot be contacted and patients whose identity cannot be established or confirmed on the basis of documents. Potentially, they could be missing persons. On the other hand, it would not be justified to notify if an unconscious person or a minor whose identity is known has been transferred to a hospital, e.g. from a work establishment, from a school or from another hospital And it is possible to communicate with her loved ones. The procedure for notifying the police will apply only to information that such a person is, without giving other data, i.e. growth, weight, gender, specific characteristics and suspected age. In order to improve the mode of transmission to the police of these data, the police are required to provide a telephone, fax or e-mail number so that in each hospital such an address exists and does not need to be searched and made available to the hospital every time. Another regulation introduced by this law is the terminology change, i.e. the replacement of the term "medical establishment' and the harmonisation of the terminology by replacing the term "negative financial result' with the term "net loss'. This project provides that a hospital, an infirmary, a sanatorium, a hospice, a care and medical facility is now called a medical facility. In the opinion of the designer, this is an unambiguous concept associated with medicinal activities. The term "Company' for the definition of a set of assets by which a medicinal entity carries out a specific type of medicinal activity is associated with a production company. The introduction of a net loss instead of a negative financial result is also a alignment of the terminology of the law with the terminology that is valid in accounting and accounting regulations. Another amendment introduced by this law is the waiver of the obligation to have medical accident insurance. The introduction of this insurance was intended to protect the interests of patients who had to deal with the voivodship committees ruling on medical events. Then the hospital, if it was insured, could easily pay those compensations. However, life showed that last year, according to the Minister, only one hospital in Poland had such insurance. That is why we waive the obligation of such insurance, although this does not mean that a hospital or a healthcare provider is exempt from liability for a medical event. Another change is the introduction of the possibility for local authorities to finance healthcare services. This change is quite fundamental, because so far the local government unit could only give subsidies to the so-called "subsidiaries." a specific objective, including investment, for the purchase of medical equipment, for the training of medical staff and for the running of preventive programmes. The provisions introduced by this law make it possible to finance healthcare services provided by healthcare providers for which they are creators. This project provides for the possibility to finance the benefits guaranteed to the members of the local government. There are some restrictions that in a sense inhibit the externalisation of these funds, since first such funding must be provided to a medicinal entity which is the responsibility of the host body. Where this establishment does not provide such services, it will be possible to provide this money and this funding will be provided to the external entity. The aim of this solution is to enable individuals to secure the best possible access to healthcare for members of the local government. On the other hand, it should also contribute to reducing the generation of losses by medicinal products. However, so that there is no double funding, i.e. in parallel by the National Health Fund and the local government unit, the proposed law allows the financing by local government units of the benefits covered by the contract for the provision of health care services only to an extent exceeding the amount of the obligation laid down in the contract, i.e. so-called. over-limit benefits. In addition, the Act imposes an obligation for the provider to inform the provider of the medicinal activity . who has concluded a contract for the provision of health care services, a branch of the voivodship of the National Health Fund. It should be stressed that the amendment introduced by the law does not impose an obligation on local government units to finance the benefits guaranteed to members of their community, but grants such an opportunity. The proposed law therefore does not impose new obligations on local authorities and therefore does not grant them new financial resources for the implementation and financing of such benefits. At the same time, this possibility is without prejudice to public authorities' obligations under Article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2nd Constitution of the Republic of Poland. In order not to prejudice access rules under this Article, the proposed amendment provides for the possibility of financing the benefits guaranteed by the local government units over the amount of the National Health Fund's commitment to the provider concerned. Another change... Mrs. Marshal, there's quite a lot of that, so I'm going to try to summarize it pretty briefly, but there's still a few points ahead of me, so please be patient. The next amendment allows the possibility to transfer the responsibilities and powers of an entity setting up an independent public health establishment between operators. This is the change that allows the local government unit to transfer its so-called. SPOZ another local government unit. E.g. transfer of SPZOZ, which is currently under the jurisdiction of the county, to the Marshal of the voivodship. This is aimed at improving activity, focusing it in one place, in one hand. This is, of course, only an opportunity, not a necessity. Another regulation allows for the creation of independent public health establishments. Until now, the bill has not provided for this. At the moment, the project assumes that the State Treasury represented by a minister, a central government authority or a voivode A local government unit and a public medical university may set up a separate health care facility. Further, the Act introduces a number of simplifications, among others. exempts managers of medicinal entities from the obligation to provide the State Labour Inspection and the competent authorities of the medical profession with information on the number of persons who pursue a medical profession under civil law agreements or as a professional practice with which the medicinal entity has concluded a civil law agreement. There's still a few minor changes, but... [[Mr. Senator, you've been reporting for over 20 minutes, so maybe...]] But it's a bill, Mrs. Marshal. I'm almost done. [[I do.]] Two more minutes and I'll be done. The Act introduces the principle that a contest for the post of deputy manager in a non-business medical entity is carried out by the head of that entity, and not, so far, by the formation entity. Mrs. Marshal, if you'll excuse me, this is the last point. The Act replaces an optional statutory authorisation for the Minister responsible for Health to issue standards of medical procedure with an authorisation to issue organisational standards. This was advocated by the professional government of doctors. He doesn't think he's a minister, but it's these medical organizations that are entitled to create medical standards. That's it, Marshal. I can finish. I ask the Senate to adopt this law without amendment.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-06-16-senat-20-2.u294	Thank you very much. In fact, at the moment, scientific societies are mainly developing standards, guidelines and recommendations, depending on their rank. But these are organisational standards, procedural standards, standards of conduct, but not clinical ones. For example, such a set of many standards are precisely organized all basket regulations, those dozens of extensive basket regulations, where these standards are defined, precisely organizational standards, hardware standards, as regards staff, of course not about clinical requirements. And indeed, at the moment, we are working to ensure that, as is the case in other countries, these recommendations and guidelines have some power. So, in short, the point is that they think it is necessary to proceed in terms of clinical behaviour, but with the possibility of certain derogations that need to be justified. It is necessary to justify a different course of action. Because it's known that... some of the state is doctors and you know that such situations aren't uncommon at all. In turn, if standards were fixed in law, then any such derogation would not be possible, then any derogation would also be a crime, in short. I don't know any country in the world that regulates clinical standards in the form of law. It is a matter of ordering them in such a way that one institution does not develop them as much as it ensures that they are up to date and that they are created under uniform procedures. And I would like to remind you that such procedures are, moreover, developed by the Council of Europe, and these are procedures for developing clinical standards. At the moment, there's no decision on which unit it is. Three versions are considered: the first is that it will be the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarification, the second is the Quality Monitoring Centre in Cracow, and the third is perhaps the medical self-government. In my opinion, this should be the Quality Monitoring Centre in Krakow, which runs the whole accreditation system, but in the future it dealt with certain standards.

The report is contained in print No 135 S. The applicant is a group of senators. Ladies and gentlemen, on 28 June 1956, metal factory workers, Cegielski's factories, went to the street. Cegielski's factories in 1956 were named after Joseph Stalin. For 3 days the break of Poznań's June lasted. 359 tanks in the streets of Poznań, many people died.... These events were the result of strikes which were caused by failure to reach an agreement as regards the demands made by the strikers. The meetings lasted, the trade unions went to Warsaw to the metal band, met at the Ministry responsible for metal industry, did not reach agreements, returned to Poznań. In a moment, I will tell you what the demands were, and at the moment I would like to move on to the resolution itself. I would first of all like to thank the chairman of the committee and the other members of the committee for the discussion and for the comments that have been made. We have made two substantive amendments, tabled by Senator Jan Żaryn, and several stylistic amendments. The first was about converting Poles to workers, because in 1956 there was a protest of workers. The second amendment, which was accepted, meant that Stanisław Hejmowski, the defender of the events of Poznań June, should be mentioned by name. He's the one who said that he doesn't feed on bullets of people who cry out for bread. In this resolution, we wanted to mention the name and surname of the defender of the events of Poznań June. Besides, there is also a street named Stanisław Hejmowski in Poznań. These two amendments have been introduced. Ladies and gentlemen, I will now read you the text of the resolution. Resolution of the Senate of the Republic of Poland of 16 June 2016 On 28 June 1956, the workers of the Cegielski factory, who were then named Joseph Stalin, stopped their work and went out into the streets. They chanted slogans: "We want to live like people," "We want free Poland," "We want religion in schools." As the march moved towards the center of the city, it grew and grew powerful. Already 100,000 people gathered before the building of the Provincial Committee of the PZPR. Because the authorities ignored the protest, the crowd broke into the building and then went to the prison and to the headquarters of the Security Office. Even though the international Poznań Fair continued in the city, the militia opened fire to demonstrators in front of witnesses from all over the world. One of the first to die was 13-year-old Romek Strzłkowski, who became a symbol of heroic struggle against communist dictatorship, enslavement of the nation and violation of the fundamental rights of the individual. There were dozens of dead, about a thousand wounded and hundreds of oppressed. The view of tanks on the streets of the city, Cyrankiewicz's threat that whoever raises his hand on the people's power, this power will chop off this hand and the beautiful words of the defender of the victims of communist terror Dr. Stanisław Hejmowski, that he does not feed on bullets of people who cry out for bread. Poznań drama 1956 opens a long list of struggles of Polish workers with communist slavery. Therefore, on a monument in the heart of the city depicting two steel crosses, symbols of death and resurrection, connected with a cord next to 1956 there are dates: 1968, 1970, 1976, 1980 and 1981. The crosses complement the monument with the head of the eagle. The Senate of the Republic of Poland on the 60th anniversary of the Poznań Uprising in 1956 pays tribute to its heroic participants. The resolution is published in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland. [[Applause]] Thank you, High Senate, and I ask you to adopt this resolution.
